z_{k}:=2\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}a_{j}^{\ast}Q_{j,n}(x),

with the convention
z_{0}:=0
. Since by
Lemma
C.5
,
|Q_{k,n}(x)|\leq 1
for every
k\in\{0,\ldots,d+1\}
, we have

|z_{k}|\leq 2\|\mathbf{a}^{\ast}\|_{1}<1,\qquad k\in\{0,\ldots,d+1\}.

(181)

For every
k\in\{0,\ldots,d\}
, let
w_{k}
denote the suffix of the
encoding beginning with the coefficient
a_{k}^{\ast}
, namely

w_{k}:=v^{(a_{k}^{\ast})}1^{n}0\,1\cdots 1\,v^{(a_{d}^{\ast})}1^{n}0\,0\,1^{k_{\mathbf{a}}}0v,

and set
w_{d+1}:=1^{k_{\mathbf{a}}}0v
. We also define

s_{k}:=\begin{cases}1w_{k+1},&k\in\{0,\ldots,d-1\},\\
0w_{d+1},&k=d.\end{cases}

Thus,

w_{k}=v^{(a_{k}^{\ast})}1^{n}0s_{k},\qquad w_{0}=u^{\mathsf{T}}_{\mathbf{a},n}v.

For every
k\in\{0,\ldots,d\}
, define

T_{{\bm{\mathsf{Hom}}},k}:=\len{v^{(a_{k}^{\ast})}},\qquad T_{{\bm{\mathsf{TStep}}}}:=2n+5,

and the following configurations of
{\bm{\mathsf{TSum}}}
:

\begin{cases}c_{k,0}&:=(1;|w_{k};x,Q_{k,n}(x),Q_{k+1,n}(x),z_{k},0_{14}),\\
c_{k,1}&:=(2;|w_{k};x,Q_{k,n}(x),Q_{k+1,n}(x),z_{k},2Q_{k,n}(x),0_{13}),\\
c_{k,2}&:=(5;|1^{n}0s_{k};x,Q_{k,n}(x),Q_{k+1,n}(x),z_{k},2a_{k}^{\ast}Q_{k,n}(x),0_{13}),\\
c_{k,3}&:=(6;|1^{n}0s_{k};x,Q_{k,n}(x),Q_{k+1,n}(x),z_{k+1},0_{14}),\\
c_{k,4}&:=(11;|s_{k};x,Q_{k+1,n}(x),Q_{k+2,n}(x),z_{k+1},0_{14}).\end{cases}

(182)

Finally, define

c_{{\bm{\mathsf{S}}}}:=(12;|w_{d+1};x,z_{d+1},0_{16}),\qquad c_{\mathrm{out}}:=(13;|v;x,H_{\mathbf{a},n}(x),0_{16}),\qquad T_{\bm{\mathsf{S}}}:=k_{\mathbf{a}}+1.

Note that
c_{0,0}=c
. Moreover,

2^{k_{\mathbf{a}}}z_{d+1}=2\sum_{j=0}^{d}a_{j}Q_{j,n}(x)=H_{\mathbf{a},n}(x),

and therefore
c_{\mathrm{out}}=c^{\prime}
.

{claim*}

For every
k\in\{0,\ldots,d\}
, the following statements hold:

(a)

{\bm{\mathsf{TSum}}}(c_{k,0})=c_{k,1}
and

\|c_{k,0}\|_{{\bm{\mathsf{TSum}}}}^{1}\leq 2
.

(b)

{\bm{\mathsf{TSum}}}^{T_{{\bm{\mathsf{Hom}}},k}}(c_{k,1})=c_{k,2}
and

\|c_{k,1}\|_{{\bm{\mathsf{TSum}}}}^{T_{{\bm{\mathsf{Hom}}},k}}\leq 2
.

(c)

{\bm{\mathsf{TSum}}}(c_{k,2})=c_{k,3}
and

\|c_{k,2}\|_{{\bm{\mathsf{TSum}}}}^{1}\leq 2
.

(d)

{\bm{\mathsf{TSum}}}^{T_{{\bm{\mathsf{TStep}}}}}(c_{k,3})=c_{k,4}
and

\|c_{k,3}\|_{{\bm{\mathsf{TSum}}}}^{T_{{\bm{\mathsf{TStep}}}}}\leq 3
.

(e)

If
k<d
, then
{\bm{\mathsf{TSum}}}(c_{k,4})=c_{k+1,0}
and

\|c_{k,4}\|_{{\bm{\mathsf{TSum}}}}^{1}\leq 1
.

Moreover,

{\bm{\mathsf{TSum}}}(c_{d,4})=c_{{\bm{\mathsf{S}}}},\qquad\|c_{d,4}\|_{{\bm{\mathsf{TSum}}}}^{1}\leq 1.

Finally,

{\bm{\mathsf{TSum}}}^{T_{\bm{\mathsf{S}}}}(c_{{\bm{\mathsf{S}}}})=c_{\mathrm{out}},\qquad\|c_{{\bm{\mathsf{S}}}}\|_{{\bm{\mathsf{TSum}}}}^{T_{\bm{\mathsf{S}}}}\leq 1\vee|H_{\mathbf{a},n}(x)|.

Proof of the Claim.

Fix
k\in\{0,\ldots,d\}
.

(a)

In configuration
c_{k,0}
, the machine is in State
1
.
Therefore, Definition
C.5
gives

{\bm{\mathsf{TSum}}}(c_{k,0})=c_{k,1}
. Moreover,

\|c_{k,0}\|_{{\bm{\mathsf{TSum}}}}^{1}\leq 2,

since
|x|,|Q_{k,n}(x)|,|Q_{k+1,n}(x)|\leq 1
,

|z_{k}|<1
, and
|2Q_{k,n}(x)|\leq 2
.

(b)

Define the configurations of
{\bm{\mathsf{Hom}}}

c_{k,1}^{\prime}:=(1;|w_{k};2Q_{k,n}(x),0),\qquad c_{k,2}^{\prime}:=(4;|1^{n}0s_{k};2a_{k}^{\ast}Q_{k,n}(x),0).

Lemma
C.2
gives

{\bm{\mathsf{Hom}}}^{T_{{\bm{\mathsf{Hom}}},k}}(c_{k,1}^{\prime})=c_{k,2}^{\prime},\qquad\|c_{k,1}^{\prime}\|_{\bm{\mathsf{Hom}}}^{T_{{\bm{\mathsf{Hom}}},k}}\leq 2|Q_{k,n}(x)|\leq 2.

Since
{\bm{\mathsf{Hom}}}
is a subroutine of
{\bm{\mathsf{TSum}}}
with correspondence

(2,3,4,5;5,6)
, and since

\operatorname{Sh}_{(2,3,4,5),(5,6)}(c_{k,1})=c_{k,1}^{\prime},\qquad\operatorname{Lift}_{(2,3,4,5),(5,6)}^{(x,Q_{k,n}(x),Q_{k+1,n}(x),z_{k},0_{12})}(c_{k,2}^{\prime})=c_{k,2},

Lemma
C.1
yields

{\bm{\mathsf{TSum}}}^{T_{{\bm{\mathsf{Hom}}},k}}(c_{k,1})=c_{k,2},\qquad\|c_{k,1}\|_{{\bm{\mathsf{TSum}}}}^{T_{{\bm{\mathsf{Hom}}},k}}\leq 2.

(c)

In configuration
c_{k,2}
, the machine is in State
5
.
Definition
C.5
therefore gives

{\bm{\mathsf{TSum}}}(c_{k,2})=c_{k,3}
, since

z_{k}+2a_{k}^{\ast}Q_{k,n}(x)=z_{k+1}.

Furthermore, (
181
) and

|2a_{k}^{\ast}Q_{k,n}(x)|\leq 2|a_{k}^{\ast}|\leq 2
imply

\|c_{k,2}\|_{{\bm{\mathsf{TSum}}}}^{1}\leq 2
.

(d)

Define the configurations of
{\bm{\mathsf{TStep}}}

c_{k,3}^{\prime}:=(1;|1^{n}0s_{k};x,Q_{k,n}(x),Q_{k+1,n}(x),0_{14}),

and

c_{k,4}^{\prime}:=(6;|s_{k};x,Q_{k+1,n}(x),Q_{k+2,n}(x),0_{14}).

Lemma
C.5
gives

{\bm{\mathsf{TStep}}}^{T_{{\bm{\mathsf{TStep}}}}}(c_{k,3}^{\prime})=c_{k,4}^{\prime},\qquad\|c_{k,3}^{\prime}\|_{{\bm{\mathsf{TStep}}}}^{T_{{\bm{\mathsf{TStep}}}}}\leq 3.

Since
{\bm{\mathsf{TStep}}}
is a subroutine of
{\bm{\mathsf{TSum}}}
with
correspondence
(6,\ldots,11;1,2,3,5,\ldots,18)
, and since

\operatorname{Sh}_{(6,\ldots,11),(1,2,3,5,\ldots,18)}(c_{k,3})=c_{k,3}^{\prime},

\operatorname{Lift}_{(6,\ldots,11),(1,2,3,5,\ldots,18)}^{z_{k+1}}(c_{k,4}^{\prime})=c_{k,4},

Lemma
C.1
yields

{\bm{\mathsf{TSum}}}^{T_{{\bm{\mathsf{TStep}}}}}(c_{k,3})=c_{k,4},\qquad\|c_{k,3}\|_{{\bm{\mathsf{TSum}}}}^{T_{{\bm{\mathsf{TStep}}}}}\leq 3.

(e)

If
k<d
, then
s_{k}=1w_{k+1}
. Thus, in configuration

c_{k,4}
, the machine is in State
11
and reads the symbol
1
.
It erases this symbol, moves right, and goes to State
1
, so

{\bm{\mathsf{TSum}}}(c_{k,4})=c_{k+1,0}
. The neural state is unchanged and
bounded by one, hence
\|c_{k,4}\|_{{\bm{\mathsf{TSum}}}}^{1}\leq 1
.

Finally,
s_{d}=0w_{d+1}
. Therefore, in configuration
c_{d,4}
,
the machine is in State
11
and reads the symbol
0
. It erases this
symbol, moves right, updates its neural state to

(x,z_{d+1},0_{16})
, and goes to State
12
. Hence,

{\bm{\mathsf{TSum}}}(c_{d,4})=c_{{\bm{\mathsf{S}}}},\qquad\|c_{d,4}\|_{{\bm{\mathsf{TSum}}}}^{1}\leq 1.

Now, define the configurations of
{\bm{\mathsf{S}}}

c_{{\bm{\mathsf{S}}}}^{\prime}:=(1;|w_{d+1};x,z_{d+1},0_{16}),\qquad c_{\mathrm{out}}^{\prime}:=(2;|v;x,H_{\mathbf{a},n}(x),0_{16}).

By Lemma
C.2
,

{\bm{\mathsf{S}}}^{T_{\bm{\mathsf{S}}}}(c_{{\bm{\mathsf{S}}}}^{\prime})=c_{\mathrm{out}}^{\prime},\qquad\|c_{{\bm{\mathsf{S}}}}^{\prime}\|_{\bm{\mathsf{S}}}^{T_{\bm{\mathsf{S}}}}\leq|H_{\mathbf{a},n}(x)|.

Since
{\bm{\mathsf{S}}}
is a subroutine of
{\bm{\mathsf{TSum}}}
with correspondence

(12,13;2)
, and since

\operatorname{Sh}_{(12,13),(2)}(c_{{\bm{\mathsf{S}}}})=c_{{\bm{\mathsf{S}}}}^{\prime},\qquad\operatorname{Lift}_{(12,13),(2)}^{(x,0_{16})}(c_{\mathrm{out}}^{\prime})=c_{\mathrm{out}},

Lemma
C.1
gives

{\bm{\mathsf{TSum}}}^{T_{\bm{\mathsf{S}}}}(c_{{\bm{\mathsf{S}}}})=c_{\mathrm{out}},\qquad\|c_{{\bm{\mathsf{S}}}}\|_{{\bm{\mathsf{TSum}}}}^{T_{\bm{\mathsf{S}}}}\leq 1\vee|H_{\mathbf{a},n}(x)|.

This concludes the proof of the claim.
∎

Repeated applications of the Chaining Lemma
C
, together
with the claim now give

{\bm{\mathsf{TSum}}}^{T_{\mathbf{a},n}}(c)=c^{\prime},\qquad\|c\|_{{\bm{\mathsf{TSum}}}}^{T_{\mathbf{a},n}}\leq 3\vee|H_{\mathbf{a},n}(x)|.

Indeed,

\displaystyle T_{\mathbf{a},n}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=0}^{d}\left(1+T_{{\bm{\mathsf{Hom}}},k}+1+T_{{\bm{\mathsf{TStep}}}}+1\right)+T_{\bm{\mathsf{S}}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=0}^{d}\left(\len{v^{(a_{k}^{\ast})}}+2n+8\right)+k_{\mathbf{a}}+1.

∎

For sequences
\mathbf{A}=(\mathbf{a}_{i})_{i\in\No}
of finite dyadic vectors and

\mathbf{n}=(n_{i})_{i\in\No}
, define

\langle\mathbf{A},\mathbf{n}\rangle_{\mathsf{T}}:=u^{\mathsf{T}}_{\mathbf{a}_{0},n_{0}}u^{\mathsf{T}}_{\mathbf{a}_{1},n_{1}}u^{\mathsf{T}}_{\mathbf{a}_{2},n_{2}}\cdots.

The continuous Chebyshev machine repeatedly executes these finite sum instructions.

{definition}

We let
{\bm{\mathsf{TC}}}
be the TMNU with neural dimension
19
and
14
states defined by the following procedure.

•

State 1.
Update the neural state as

\omega\leftarrow(\omega_{1},\tfrac{1}{2},\tfrac{\omega_{1}}{2},0_{15},\omega_{2}),

and go to State
2
.

•

States 2–13.
Use
{\bm{\mathsf{TSum}}}
as a subroutine with correspondence

(2,\ldots,13,1;1,\ldots,18)
.

•

State 14.
Halt.

The following lemma records one cycle and the resulting output convention.

{lemma}

Let
\mathbf{A}=(\mathbf{a}_{i})_{i\in\No}
be a sequence of finite dyadic
vectors, let
\mathbf{n}=(n_{i})_{i\in\No}
, and let
x\in[-1,1]
.
For every
i\in\No
, write

\mathbf{a}_{i}=(a_{i,0},\ldots,a_{i,d_{i}})
, and assume that

d_{i}+1\leq 2^{n_{i}}.

Define the tails

\mathbf{A}_{\geq i}:=(\mathbf{a}_{i+j})_{j\in\No},\qquad\mathbf{n}_{\geq i}:=(n_{i+j})_{j\in\No},

and use the conventions

H_{\mathbf{a}_{-1},n_{-1}}(x):=H_{\mathbf{a}_{-2},n_{-2}}(x):=0.

For every
i\in\No
, define the configuration

c_{i}:=\left(1;|\langle\mathbf{A}_{\geq i},\mathbf{n}_{\geq i}\rangle_{\mathsf{T}};x,H_{\mathbf{a}_{i-1},n_{i-1}}(x),0_{16},H_{\mathbf{a}_{i-2},n_{i-2}}(x)\right),

and set

T_{i}:=T_{\mathbf{a}_{i},n_{i}}+1,

where
T_{\mathbf{a}_{i},n_{i}}
is as in (
180
). Then, for every
i\in\No
,

{\bm{\mathsf{TC}}}^{T_{i}}(c_{i})=c_{i+1},

(183)

and

\|c_{i}\|_{{\bm{\mathsf{TC}}}}^{T_{i}}\leq 3\vee|H_{\mathbf{a}_{i-2},n_{i-2}}(x)|\vee|H_{\mathbf{a}_{i-1},n_{i-1}}(x)|\vee|H_{\mathbf{a}_{i},n_{i}}(x)|.

(184)

In particular,
c_{0}=(1;|\langle\mathbf{A},\mathbf{n}\rangle_{\mathsf{T}};x,0_{18})
.
By denoting

N_{i}:=\sum_{j=0}^{i-1}T_{j},

we have
{\bm{\mathsf{TC}}}^{N_{i}}(c_{0})=c_{i}
for every
i\in\No
, and

{\bm{\mathsf{TC}}}^{\langle\mathbf{A},\mathbf{n}\rangle_{\mathsf{T}}}x[t]=H_{\mathbf{a}_{i-1},n_{i-1}}(x),\qquad t\in\{N_{i}+1,\ldots,N_{i+1}\}.

(185)

Proof.

Fix
i\in\No
, and define

h_{j}:=H_{\mathbf{a}_{j},n_{j}}(x),\qquad j\in\No,

together with the conventions
h_{-1}:=h_{-2}:=0
. We also define the
intermediate configuration

\widetilde{c}_{i}:=\left(2;|\langle\mathbf{A}_{\geq i},\mathbf{n}_{\geq i}\rangle_{\mathsf{T}};x,\frac{1}{2},\frac{x}{2},0_{15},h_{i-1}\right).

We prove separately the initialization transition and the

{\bm{\mathsf{TSum}}}
subroutine stage.

{claim*}

The following statements hold:

(a)

{\bm{\mathsf{TC}}}(c_{i})=\widetilde{c}_{i}
and

\|c_{i}\|_{{\bm{\mathsf{TC}}}}^{1}\leq 1\vee|h_{i-2}|\vee|h_{i-1}|.

(b)

{\bm{\mathsf{TC}}}^{T_{\mathbf{a}_{i},n_{i}}}(\widetilde{c}_{i})=c_{i+1},

and

\|\widetilde{c}_{i}\|_{{\bm{\mathsf{TC}}}}^{T_{\mathbf{a}_{i},n_{i}}}\leq 3\vee|h_{i-1}|\vee|h_{i}|,

where
T_{\mathbf{a}_{i},n_{i}}
is as in (
180
). Moreover, for every

s\in\{0,\ldots,T_{\mathbf{a}_{i},n_{i}}\}
,

\pi_{19}\pi_{\omega}{\bm{\mathsf{TC}}}^{s}(\widetilde{c}_{i})=h_{i-1}.

(186)

Proof of the Claim.

We prove both statements in turn.

(a)

In configuration
c_{i}
, the machine is in State
1
.
Therefore, by Definition
C.5
, it
preserves
x
, initializes the first two normalized Chebyshev
values as
1/2
and
x/2
, sets the accumulator and the remaining
working coordinates to zero, copies
h_{i-1}
into coordinate

19
, and goes to State
2
. Hence,

{\bm{\mathsf{TC}}}(c_{i})=\widetilde{c}_{i}.

Since
|x|\leq 1
, we also have

\displaystyle\|c_{i}\|_{{\bm{\mathsf{TC}}}}^{1}

\displaystyle\leq\|(x,h_{i-1},0_{16},h_{i-2})\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\quad\vee\left\|\left(x,\frac{1}{2},\frac{x}{2},0_{15},h_{i-1}\right)\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq 1\vee|h_{i-2}|\vee|h_{i-1}|.

(b)

Define the configurations of
{\bm{\mathsf{TSum}}}

\widetilde{c}_{i}^{\prime}:=\left(1;|\langle\mathbf{A}_{\geq i},\mathbf{n}_{\geq i}\rangle_{\mathsf{T}};x,\frac{1}{2},\frac{x}{2},0_{15}\right),

and

c_{i+1}^{\prime}:=\left(13;|\langle\mathbf{A}_{\geq i+1},\mathbf{n}_{\geq i+1}\rangle_{\mathsf{T}};x,h_{i},0_{16}\right).

Since

\langle\mathbf{A}_{\geq i},\mathbf{n}_{\geq i}\rangle_{\mathsf{T}}=u^{\mathsf{T}}_{\mathbf{a}_{i},n_{i}}\langle\mathbf{A}_{\geq i+1},\mathbf{n}_{\geq i+1}\rangle_{\mathsf{T}},

Lemma
C.5
gives

{\bm{\mathsf{TSum}}}^{T_{\mathbf{a}_{i},n_{i}}}(\widetilde{c}_{i}^{\prime})=c_{i+1}^{\prime},

and

\|\widetilde{c}_{i}^{\prime}\|_{{\bm{\mathsf{TSum}}}}^{T_{\mathbf{a}_{i},n_{i}}}\leq 3\vee|h_{i}|.

By Definition
C.5
,
{\bm{\mathsf{TSum}}}
is a
subroutine of
{\bm{\mathsf{TC}}}
with correspondence

(2,\ldots,13,1;1,\ldots,18)
. Furthermore,

\operatorname{Sh}_{(2,\ldots,13,1),(1,\ldots,18)}(\widetilde{c}_{i})=\widetilde{c}_{i}^{\prime},

and

\operatorname{Lift}_{(2,\ldots,13,1),(1,\ldots,18)}^{h_{i-1}}(c_{i+1}^{\prime})=c_{i+1}.

Therefore, Lemma
C.1
yields

{\bm{\mathsf{TC}}}^{T_{\mathbf{a}_{i},n_{i}}}(\widetilde{c}_{i})=c_{i+1},

and

\|\widetilde{c}_{i}\|_{{\bm{\mathsf{TC}}}}^{T_{\mathbf{a}_{i},n_{i}}}\leq\|\widetilde{c}_{i}^{\prime}\|_{{\bm{\mathsf{TSum}}}}^{T_{\mathbf{a}_{i},n_{i}}}\vee\|\widetilde{c}_{i}\|\leq 3\vee|h_{i-1}|\vee|h_{i}|.

Since coordinate
19
is not used by the
{\bm{\mathsf{TSum}}}
subroutine,
the same application of Lemma
C.1
gives
(
186
).

This concludes the proof of the claim.
∎

Since
T_{i}=1+T_{\mathbf{a}_{i},n_{i}}
, the Chaining Lemma

C
and the claim give

{\bm{\mathsf{TC}}}^{T_{i}}(c_{i})=c_{i+1},

and

\|c_{i}\|_{{\bm{\mathsf{TC}}}}^{T_{i}}\leq 3\vee|h_{i-2}|\vee|h_{i-1}|\vee|h_{i}|.

This proves (
183
) and
(
184
). We now prove the last statements. By induction on
i
,

{\bm{\mathsf{TC}}}^{N_{i}}(c_{0})=c_{i},\qquad i\in\No.

Fix
i\in\No
and

t\in\{N_{i}+1,\ldots,N_{i+1}\}
. Then

s:=t-N_{i}-1\in\{0,\ldots,T_{\mathbf{a}_{i},n_{i}}\}.

Using
{\bm{\mathsf{TC}}}^{N_{i}}(c_{0})=c_{i}
and

{\bm{\mathsf{TC}}}(c_{i})=\widetilde{c}_{i}
, we obtain

{\bm{\mathsf{TC}}}^{t}(c_{0})={\bm{\mathsf{TC}}}^{s}(\widetilde{c}_{i}).

Therefore, by Definition
4.3
and
(
186
),

{\bm{\mathsf{TC}}}^{\langle\mathbf{A},\mathbf{n}\rangle_{\mathsf{T}}}x[t]=\pi_{19}\pi_{\omega}{\bm{\mathsf{TC}}}^{s}(\widetilde{c}_{i})=h_{i-1}.

This proves (
185
).
∎

We can now balance degree and precision to obtain the native Chebyshev rate.

{theorem}
[Native Chebyshev tradeoff]
Let
f:[-1,1]\to\R
be a Dini-continuous continuous function. Let

\eta:[1,\infty)\to
be nondecreasing, with

\eta(t)\to\infty
and
t/\eta(t)
nondecreasing. Suppose that

\eta(t)\log_{2}(\eta(t)+1)\leq At,\qquad t\geq 1,

(187)

for some
A>0
. Define

B_{f}:=2\|f\|_{L^{\infty}([-1,1])}+1,\qquad\gamma_{f}:=\left\lceil\log_{2}(B_{f})\right\rceil,

and

\tau:=\tau_{f,A}:=4\bigl(10+\gamma_{f}+A(80+6\gamma_{f})\bigr).

(188)

Then, there exists
u\in\{0,1\}
such that

(1;|u;x,0_{18})\in\Bc_{{\bm{\mathsf{TC}}}}^{\infty}\left(3+S(f,0)+\|f\|_{L^{\infty}([-1,1])}\right),\qquad x\in[-1,1],

and, for every
x\in[-1,1]
and
t\geq\tau
,

\left|{\bm{\mathsf{TC}}}^{u}x[t]-f(x)\right|\leq 2\cdot 2^{-\frac{t/\tau}{\eta(t/\tau)}}+S\left(f,\eta(t/\tau)\right),

(189)

where
S(f,d)
is as in (
14
).

Proof.

First note that since
f
is DiniLipschitz continuous,
S(f,d)\to 0
as
d\to\infty
and
S(f,d)<\infty
for every
d\in\No
. For every
i\in\No
, set

d_{i}:=\eta(2^{i}),\quad q_{i}:=\left\lceil\frac{2^{i}}{d_{i}}\right\rceil,\quad\ell_{i}:=\left\lceil\log_{2}(d_{i}+1)\right\rceil,\quad p_{i}:=q_{i}+\ell_{i}+2,\quad n_{i}:=\left\lceil\frac{q_{i}+3\ell_{i}+\gamma_{f}}{2}\right\rceil,

(190)

and for
k\in\{0,\ldots,d_{i}\}
, choose

a_{i,k}\in 2^{-p_{i}}\Z
such that

|a_{i,k}-c_{k}^{(f)}|\leq 2^{-p_{i}},

(191)

where
c_{k}^{(f)}
is the
k
-th Chebyshev coefficient of
f
,
and set

\mathbf{a}_{i}:=(a_{i,0},\ldots,a_{i,d_{i}}),\qquad\mathbf{A}:=(\mathbf{a}_{i})_{i\in\No},\qquad\mathbf{n}:=(n_{i})_{i\in\No},\qquad u:=\langle\mathbf{A},\mathbf{n}\rangle_{\mathsf{T}}.

The proof is effected by estimating the error and duration of each cycle of the computation.

(a)

We first estimate the error of cycle
i\in\No
.
Note that
d_{i}+1\leq 2^{\ell_{i}}\leq 2^{n_{i}}
, so Lemma
C.5
applies. Therefore, we get

\displaystyle\hskip-14.22636pt\left|H_{\mathbf{a}_{i},n_{i}}(x)-S^{(f)}_{d_{i}}(x)\right|

\displaystyle=\left|2\sum_{k=0}^{d_{i}}a_{i,k}Q_{n_{i},k}(x)-\sum_{k=0}^{d_{i}}c_{k}^{(f)}T_{k}(x)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left|2\sum_{k=0}^{d_{i}}a_{i,k}Q_{n_{i},k}(x)-2\sum_{k=0}^{d_{i}}c_{k}^{(f)}Q_{n_{i},k}(x)\right|+\left|2\sum_{k=0}^{d_{i}}c_{k}^{(f)}Q_{n_{i},k}(x)-\sum_{k=0}^{d_{i}}c_{k}^{(f)}T_{k}(x)\right|

\displaystyle\leq 2\sum_{k=0}^{d_{i}}|a_{i,k}-c_{k}^{(f)}|+2\sum_{k=0}^{d_{i}}|c_{k}^{(f)}|\left|Q_{n_{i},k}(x)-\frac{T_{k}(x)}{2}\right|

\displaystyle\overset{(a)}{\leq}2d_{i}2^{-p_{i}}+B_{f}d_{i}^{2}2^{-2n_{i}-1}\overset{\eqref{eq:native-chebychev-parameters}}{\leq}2^{-q_{i}-1}+2^{-q_{i}-1}=2^{-q_{i}},

where (a) follows from Lemma
C.5
and (
191
). Hence,

|H_{\mathbf{a}_{i},n_{i}}(x)-f(x)|\leq 2^{-q_{i}}+S(f,d_{i}),\qquad x\in[-1,1].

(192)

(b)

We now derive an explicit bound on the duration of cycle
i
. First note that

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{a}_{i}\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k=0}^{d_{i}}(|a_{i,k}-c_{k}^{(f)}|+|c_{k}^{(f)}|)\overset{\eqref{eq:approx-cheb-coeffs:proof:native-tradeoff}}{\leq}(d_{i}+1)\left(2^{-p_{i}}+\max_{k\in\{0,\ldots,d_{i}\}}|c_{k}^{(f)}|\right)

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{eq:chebyshev-coefficients}}{\leq}(d_{i}+1)\left(1+2\|f\|_{L^{\infty}([-1,1])}\right)=(d_{i}+1)B_{f}.

(193)

From
(
(b)
) and the definition (
179
) of

k_{\mathbf{a}_{i}}
, we have

k_{\mathbf{a}_{i}}\leq\ell_{i}+\gamma_{f}+2.

(194)

Since
a_{i,k}\in 2^{-p_{i}}\Z
, we have

a_{i,k}^{\ast}\in 2^{-p_{i}-k_{\mathbf{a}_{i}}}\Z\cap\Db_{1}
. Therefore,

\len{v^{(a_{i,k}^{\ast})}}\overset{\eqref{eq:len-enc-dya-sign}}{\leq}2(p_{i}+k_{\mathbf{a}_{i}})+2.

(195)

Let
T_{i}:=T_{\mathbf{a}_{i},n_{i}}+1
be the duration of cycle
i
in

{\bm{\mathsf{TC}}}
, where
T_{\mathbf{a}_{i},n_{i}}
is as in (
180
). By
(
194
), and
(
195
),

\displaystyle T_{i}

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{eq:cheb-sum-time-bound}}{\leq}1+\sum_{k=0}^{d_{i}}\left(\len{v^{(a_{i,k}^{\ast})}}+2n_{i}+8\right)+k_{\mathbf{a}_{i}}+1

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{eq:bound-encoding-ai:proof:native-tradeoff}}{\leq}(d_{i}+1)(2p_{i}+2k_{\mathbf{a}_{i}}+2n_{i}+10)+k_{\mathbf{a}_{i}}+2

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{eq:bound-k-ai:proof:native-tradeoff}}{\leq}(d_{i}+1)(2p_{i}+2(\ell_{i}+\gamma_{f}+2)+2n_{i}+10)+\ell_{i}+\gamma_{f}+2+2

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{eq:native-chebychev-parameters}}{\leq}(d_{i}+1)\left(3q_{i}+7\ell_{i}+3\gamma_{f}+22\right)+\ell_{i}+\gamma_{f}+4.

(196)

We bound each term in (
196
).
First, since
d_{i}=\eta(2^{i})\geq 1
, (
187
) gives

d_{i}\log_{2}(d_{i}+1)\leq A2^{i},\qquad d_{i}\leq\frac{A2^{i}}{\log_{2}(d_{i}+1)}\leq\frac{A2^{i}}{\log_{2}(2)}=A2^{i},\qquad d_{i}+1\leq 2d_{i}\leq 2A2^{i}.

(197)

Moreover, by (
190
),

q_{i}\leq\frac{2^{i}}{d_{i}}+1,\qquad\ell_{i}\leq\log_{2}(d_{i}+1)+1\leq 2\log_{2}(d_{i}+1)\leq 2A2^{i}.

(198)

which yields the two product bounds

\displaystyle(d_{i}+1)q_{i}

\displaystyle\leq 2d_{i}\left(\frac{2^{i}}{d_{i}}+1\right)=2\cdot 2^{i}+2d_{i}\leq(2+2A)2^{i},

(199)

\displaystyle(d_{i}+1)\ell_{i}

\displaystyle\leq 4d_{i}\log_{2}(d_{i}+1)\leq 4A2^{i}.

(200)

Substituting (
197
), (
198
), (
199
) and (
200
) into
(
196
), and using

\gamma_{f}+4\leq(\gamma_{f}+4)2^{i}
, gives

\displaystyle T_{i}

\displaystyle\leq\bigl(3(2+2A)+7(4A)+(3\gamma_{f}+22)(2A)+2A+\gamma_{f}+4\bigr)2^{i}

\displaystyle=\bigl(10+\gamma_{f}+A(80+6\gamma_{f})\bigr)2^{i}=:\frac{\tau}{4}2^{i}.

(201)

Hence, with the notation of Lemma
C.5
,

N_{i}+1=\sum_{j=0}^{i-1}T_{j}+1\leq\frac{\tau}{4}(2^{i}-1)+1\leq\frac{\tau}{4}2^{i},

(202)

where the last inequality uses
\tau/4\geq 10/4\geq 1
.

We now express the error in terms of machine time. Fix
t\geq\tau
,
and set

i:=\left\lfloor\log_{2}(4t/\tau)\right\rfloor.

Then

i\geq 2,\qquad N_{i}+1\leq\frac{\tau}{4}2^{i}\leq t,\qquad\text{and}\qquad 2^{i-1}\geq\frac{t}{\tau}.

(203)

In particular,
t\in\{N_{j}+1,\ldots,N_{j+1}\}
for some
j\geq i
, so by Lemma
C.5
,

\displaystyle|{\bm{\mathsf{TC}}}^{u}x[t]-f(x)|

\displaystyle=|H_{\mathbf{a}_{j-1},n_{j-1}}(x)-f(x)|\overset{\eqref{eq:native-cycle-error}}{\leq}2^{-q_{j-1}}+S(f,d_{j-1})\leq\sup_{k\geq i-1}\left(2^{-q_{k}}+S(f,d_{k})\right)

(204)

\displaystyle=\sup_{k\geq i-1}\left(2^{-\frac{2^{k}}{\eta(2^{k})}}+S(f,\eta(2^{k}))\right)\overset{(a)}{\leq}2^{-\frac{2^{i-1}}{\eta(2^{i-1)}}}+S(f,\eta(2^{i-1})),

(205)

where (a) follows from the fact that
\eta(t)
and
t/\eta(t)
are nondecreasing, and
t\mapsto 2^{-t}
and
t\mapsto S(f,t)
are nonincreasing. By (
203
),

d_{i-1}=\eta(2^{i-1})\geq\eta(t/\tau),\qquad\text{and}\qquad q_{i-1}\geq\left\lceil\frac{2^{i-1}}{\eta(2^{i-1})}\right\rceil\geq\frac{t/\tau}{\eta(t/\tau)}.

Substituting these inequalities into
(
204
) proves
(
189
). Finally, by (
192
), for every
i\in\No
and
x\in[-1,1]
,

|H_{\mathbf{a}_{i},n_{i}}(x)|\leq|H_{\mathbf{a}_{i},n_{i}}(x)-f(x)|+|f(x)|\leq 1+S(f,d_{i})+\|f\|_{L^{\infty}([-1,1])}\leq 1+S(f,0)+\|f\|_{L^{\infty}([-1,1])}

Lemma
C.5
therefore gives

(1;|u;x,0_{18})\in\Bc_{{\bm{\mathsf{TC}}}}^{\infty}\left(3+S(f,0)+\|f\|_{L^{\infty}([-1,1])}\right).

∎

C.6
Magnitude estimates for the TMNUs used in the constructions

We close the appendix by recording the estimates on the quantity

\|{\bf M}\|_{C}
that enters Theorem

5
. Recall from Section

5
that, if

\Fc_{\bf M}=\{f_{1},\ldots,f_{\nu_{{\bf M}}}\}
denotes the set of
command maps of a TMNU
{\bf M}
with neural dimension
d
, then

\|{\bf M}\|_{C}:=\max_{1\leq j\leq\nu_{{\bf M}}}\sup_{\omega\in[-C,C]^{d}}\|f_{j}(\omega)\|_{\infty}.

We first introduce a global magnitude for command maps. If

W:\R^{d}\to\R^{d}
is affine, say
W(\omega)=A\omega+b
, set

\mathfrak{m}(W):=\max_{1\leq i\leq d}\left(\sum_{j=1}^{d}|A_{ij}|+|b_{i}|\right).

If
W=\rho_{S}\in\mathcal{R}_{d}
, set
\mathfrak{m}(W):=1
. Finally, for a
TMNU
{\bf M}
, define

\mathfrak{m}({\bf M}):=1\vee\max_{f\in\Fc_{\bf M}}\mathfrak{m}(f).

This global command magnitude controls the bounded-region quantity used in the simulation theorem.

{lemma}

Let
{\bf M}
be a TMNU. Then, for every
C>0
,

\|{\bf M}\|_{C}\leq\mathfrak{m}({\bf M})(C\vee 1).

Proof.

Let
f\in\Fc_{\bf M}
and
\omega\in[-C,C]^{d}
. If
f=\rho_{S}
for some

S\subseteq\{1,\ldots,d\}
, then

\|f(\omega)\|_{\infty}\leq\|\omega\|_{\infty}\leq C\leq C\vee 1
.
If
f(\omega)=A\omega+b
is affine, then

\|f(\omega)\|_{\infty}\leq\max_{1\leq i\leq d}\left(\sum_{j=1}^{d}|A_{ij}|\,C+|b_{i}|\right)\leq\mathfrak{m}(f)(C\vee 1).

Taking the maximum over
f\in\Fc_{\bf M}
proves the claim.
∎

The next estimate explains how this magnitude behaves when a machine is built from a subroutine.

{lemma}

Let
{\bf N}
be a subroutine of
{\bf M}
with correspondence

(\iota_{q},\iota_{\omega})
, and define the set of states of

{\bf M}
that do not simulate non-halting states of
{\bf N}
by

Q_{\mathrm{rem}}:=\{1,\ldots,n_{\bf M}\}\setminus\iota_{q}(\{1,\ldots,n_{\bf N}-1\}).

Then, with the convention that the maximum over an empty set is
0
,

\mathfrak{m}({\bf M})\leq\mathfrak{m}({\bf N})\vee 1\vee\max_{q\in Q_{\mathrm{rem}},\,\sigma\in\Gamma}\mathfrak{m}\!\left(\kappa_{\bf M}(q,\sigma)\right).

Proof.

For a command map
f:\R^{d_{\bf N}}\to\R^{d_{\bf N}}
, let

\widetilde{f}:\R^{d_{\bf M}}\to\R^{d_{\bf M}}
be the map
obtained by applying
f
to the coordinates indexed by

\iota_{\omega}
and by leaving all complementary coordinates unchanged,
i.e.,

\pi_{\iota_{\omega}}\widetilde{f}(\omega)=f(\pi_{\iota_{\omega}}\omega),\qquad\pi_{\iota_{\omega}}^{\perp}\widetilde{f}(\omega)=\pi_{\iota_{\omega}}^{\perp}\omega.

(206)

Note that

\mathfrak{m}(\widetilde{f})\leq\mathfrak{m}(f)\vee 1.

(207)

Now fix
q\in\{1,\ldots,n_{\bf N}-1\}
and

\sigma\in\Gamma
, and set
f:=\kappa_{\bf N}(q,\sigma)
.
Let
\widetilde{f}:\R^{d_{\bf M}}\to\R^{d_{\bf M}}
be the extension of

f
defined by (
206
). By the
subroutine identity (
130
),

\kappa_{\bf M}(\iota_{q}(q),\sigma)=\widetilde{f}.

Hence, by (
207
),

\mathfrak{m}\!\left(\kappa_{\bf M}(\iota_{q}(q),\sigma)\right)\leq\mathfrak{m}\!\left(\kappa_{\bf N}(q,\sigma)\right)\vee 1\leq\mathfrak{m}({\bf N})\vee 1.

The remaining command maps of
{\bf M}
are exactly those indexed by

q\in Q_{\mathrm{rem}}
and
\sigma\in\Gamma
. Taking the
maximum over all command maps of
{\bf M}
, and recalling the outer
maximum with
1
in the definition of
\mathfrak{m}({\bf M})
, gives the
desired estimate.
∎

We finish by tabulating the command magnitudes for every machine used above.

{lemma}

The TMNUs used in the monomial and native Chebyshev constructions
satisfy

\begin{array}[]{c|ccccccccccc}{\bf M}&{\boldsymbol{{\pm}}}&{\bm{\mathsf{S}}}&{\bm{\mathsf{Hom}}}^{+}&{\boldsymbol{\times}}&{\bm{\mathsf{Hom}}}&\bm{\mathsf{\pi}}&{\bm{\mathsf{\Pi}}}&{\bm{\mathsf{C}}}&{\bm{\mathsf{TStep}}}&{\bm{\mathsf{TSum}}}&{\bm{\mathsf{TC}}}\\
\hline\cr n_{\bf M}&2&2&3&4&4&9&11&12&6&13&14\\
d_{\bf M}&1&1&2&14&2&16&16&17&17&18&19\\
\nu_{{\bf M}}&2&2&4&5&5&11&13&14&7&15&16\\
\mathfrak{m}({\bf M})&1&2&2&\leq 5&\leq 2&\leq 5&\leq 5&\leq 5&\leq 5&\leq 5&\leq 5.\end{array}

In particular, for every
C>0
,

\|{\bm{\mathsf{C}}}\|_{C}\leq 5(C\vee 1),\qquad\|{\bm{\mathsf{TC}}}\|_{C}\leq 5(C\vee 1).

Proof.

The rows for
n_{\bf M}
and
d_{\bf M}
are read directly
from Definitions
C.2
,
C.2
,

C.2
,
C.2
,
C.2
,

C.3
,
C.3
,

C.4
,
C.5
,

C.5
, and

C.5
. For
{\boldsymbol{\times}}
, Definition

C.2
identifies it with the TMNU associated to the
multiplication RNN of Lemma
C.2
, which has hidden
dimension
14
; the TMNU construction of Definition

C.2
has
4
states.

We next count the distinct command maps. By inspection of Definitions

C.2
,
C.2
, and

C.2
, the elementary machines have

\nu_{{\boldsymbol{{\pm}}}}=2,\qquad\nu_{{\bm{\mathsf{S}}}}=2,\qquad\nu_{{\bm{\mathsf{Hom}}}^{+}}=4.

Moreover, Definition
C.2
gives five command maps
for a simulated RNN, and therefore

\nu_{{\boldsymbol{\times}}}=5
. When a machine is built by inserting subroutines,
we count the union of the extended command maps of the subroutines and the
additional explicit command maps; the identity command is counted only
once. Inspection of the coordinates on which the non-identity parts act
shows that there are no further coincidences between the command maps
listed below.
Thus

\nu_{{\bm{\mathsf{Hom}}}}=\nu_{{\boldsymbol{{\pm}}}}+\nu_{{\bm{\mathsf{Hom}}}^{+}}-1=5.

The machine
\bm{\mathsf{\pi}}
adds two explicit commands to the
{\bm{\mathsf{Hom}}}
and

{\boldsymbol{\times}}
subroutines, whence

\nu_{\bm{\mathsf{\pi}}}=\nu_{{\bm{\mathsf{Hom}}}}+\nu_{{\boldsymbol{\times}}}-1+2=11.

Similarly,
{\bm{\mathsf{\Pi}}}
adds one explicit command to the
\bm{\mathsf{\pi}}
and
{\bm{\mathsf{S}}}

subroutines, and
{\bm{\mathsf{C}}}
adds one explicit command to the
{\bm{\mathsf{\Pi}}}

subroutine. Hence

\nu_{{\bm{\mathsf{\Pi}}}}=13,\qquad\nu_{{\bm{\mathsf{C}}}}=14.

On the native Chebyshev side,
{\bm{\mathsf{TStep}}}
adds two explicit commands to
the
{\boldsymbol{\times}}
subroutine, so

\nu_{{\bm{\mathsf{TStep}}}}=5+2=7.

The machine
{\bm{\mathsf{TSum}}}
combines the
{\bm{\mathsf{Hom}}}
,
{\bm{\mathsf{TStep}}}
, and
{\bm{\mathsf{S}}}

subroutines and adds three explicit commands: the initialization command,
the accumulation command, and the exit command before the final scaling
subroutine. Therefore

\nu_{{\bm{\mathsf{TSum}}}}=\nu_{{\bm{\mathsf{Hom}}}}+(\nu_{{\bm{\mathsf{TStep}}}}-1)+(\nu_{{\bm{\mathsf{S}}}}-1)+3=15.

Finally,
{\bm{\mathsf{TC}}}
adds one explicit initialization command to the

{\bm{\mathsf{TSum}}}
subroutine, and hence

\nu_{{\bm{\mathsf{TC}}}}=16.

It remains to prove the command-magnitude row. We inspect the command maps
in the order in which the machines are constructed. Throughout, the command
maps that are not explicitly listed are identities, or frozen complementary
coordinates coming from subroutines, and therefore have magnitude
1
.
When a machine contains several disjoint subroutine blocks, we apply Lemma

C.6
successively to these blocks and
then take the maximum with the magnitudes of the remaining explicit
commands.

•

For
{\boldsymbol{{\pm}}}
, the only nontrivial command is

\omega\mapsto\pm\omega
, hence
\mathfrak{m}({\boldsymbol{{\pm}}})=1
.

•

For
{\bm{\mathsf{S}}}
, the nontrivial command is

\omega\mapsto 2\omega
, while all other commands are the identity.
Hence
\mathfrak{m}({\bm{\mathsf{S}}})=2
.

•

For
{\bm{\mathsf{Hom}}}^{+}
, the possible affine commands are

(\omega_{1},\omega_{2})\mapsto(\omega_{2},0),\qquad(\omega_{1},\omega_{2})\mapsto(\tfrac{1}{2}\omega_{1},\omega_{2}),

and

(\omega_{1},\omega_{2})\mapsto(\omega_{1},\omega_{2}+b\omega_{1}),\qquad b\in\{\square,0,1\}.

Since
\square=-1
, these maps have magnitudes at most
1
,

1
, and
2
, respectively. Thus

\mathfrak{m}({\bm{\mathsf{Hom}}}^{+})=2
.

•

For
{\boldsymbol{\times}}
, we inspect of the RNN multiplication
construction used in Lemma
C.2
. This RNN was introduced in
[
14
, Theorem 11]
. This yields

\mathfrak{m}({\boldsymbol{\times}})\leq 5
.

•

The machine
{\bm{\mathsf{Hom}}}
uses
{\boldsymbol{{\pm}}}
and
{\bm{\mathsf{Hom}}}^{+}
as subroutines
and has no additional nontrivial command map. Lemma

C.6
therefore gives

\mathfrak{m}({\bm{\mathsf{Hom}}})\leq\mathfrak{m}({\boldsymbol{{\pm}}})\vee\mathfrak{m}({\bm{\mathsf{Hom}}}^{+})\vee 1=2.

•

The explicit commands of
\bm{\mathsf{\pi}}
are

\omega\mapsto(\omega_{1},\omega_{2},\omega_{3},\omega_{2},0_{12})

and

\omega\mapsto(\omega_{1},\omega_{2},\omega_{3}+\omega_{4},\omega_{1},0_{12}),

with magnitudes
1
and
2
. Its subroutines are
{\bm{\mathsf{Hom}}}
and

{\boldsymbol{\times}}
, hence

\mathfrak{m}(\bm{\mathsf{\pi}})\leq 2\vee\mathfrak{m}({\bm{\mathsf{Hom}}})\vee\mathfrak{m}({\boldsymbol{\times}})\leq 5.

•

The only explicit nontrivial command of
{\bm{\mathsf{\Pi}}}
outside its
subroutines is

\omega\mapsto(\omega_{1},\omega_{3},0_{14}),

which has magnitude
1
. Its subroutines are
\bm{\mathsf{\pi}}
and
{\bm{\mathsf{S}}}
,
so

\mathfrak{m}({\bm{\mathsf{\Pi}}})\leq 1\vee\mathfrak{m}(\bm{\mathsf{\pi}})\vee\mathfrak{m}({\bm{\mathsf{S}}})\leq 5.

•

The explicit initialization command of
{\bm{\mathsf{C}}}
is

\omega\mapsto(\omega_{1},1,0_{14},\omega_{2}),

which has magnitude
1
. Since the only subroutine is
{\bm{\mathsf{\Pi}}}
,

\mathfrak{m}({\bm{\mathsf{C}}})\leq 1\vee\mathfrak{m}({\bm{\mathsf{\Pi}}})\leq 5.

•

For
{\bm{\mathsf{TStep}}}
, the explicit commands are

\omega\mapsto(\omega_{1},\omega_{2},\omega_{3},\omega_{1},\omega_{3},0_{12})

and

\omega\mapsto(\omega_{1},\omega_{3},2\omega_{4}-\omega_{2},0_{14}),

with magnitudes
1
and
3
. The only subroutine is
{\boldsymbol{\times}}
, hence

\mathfrak{m}({\bm{\mathsf{TStep}}})\leq 5
.

•

For
{\bm{\mathsf{TSum}}}
, the explicit commands outside subroutines have
magnitudes at most
2
: the initialization command has the row

2\omega_{2}
, the accumulation command has the row

\omega_{4}+\omega_{5}
, and the exit command

\omega\mapsto(\omega_{1},\omega_{4},0_{16})
has magnitude
1
. Its
subroutines are
{\bm{\mathsf{Hom}}}
,
{\bm{\mathsf{TStep}}}
, and
{\bm{\mathsf{S}}}
, so

\mathfrak{m}({\bm{\mathsf{TSum}}})\leq 2\vee\mathfrak{m}({\bm{\mathsf{Hom}}})\vee\mathfrak{m}({\bm{\mathsf{TStep}}})\vee\mathfrak{m}({\bm{\mathsf{S}}})\leq 5.

•

Finally, the explicit initialization command of

{\bm{\mathsf{TC}}}
is

\omega\mapsto(\omega_{1},\tfrac{1}{2},\tfrac{\omega_{1}}{2},0_{15},\omega_{2}),

which has magnitude at most
1
. The only subroutine is
{\bm{\mathsf{TSum}}}
,
and therefore
\mathfrak{m}({\bm{\mathsf{TC}}})\leq 5
.

The two displayed bounds for
\|\cdot\|_{C}
are now immediate from Lemma

C.6
.
∎

Appendix D
Technical results on approximation of polynomials with RNNs

We begin with the monomial estimate produced by repeated approximate multiplication.

{lemma}

For all
d,n\in\No
and
x\in[-1,1]
, we have

\left|G_{d,n}(x)-x^{d}\right|\leq d2^{-2n-1}.

and

|G_{d,n}(x)|\leq 1.

Proof.

We prove the first statement by induction on
d
.

(a)

Base case
d=0
. For all
n\in\No
and
x\in[-1,1]
, we have

|G_{0,n}(x)-x^{0}|=|1-1|=0\leq 0\cdot 2^{-2n-1}.

(b)

Inductive step. Assume that the statement holds for some
d\in\No
. We prove that it also holds for
d+1
. Let
n\in\No
and
x\in[-1,1]
. We have

\displaystyle|G_{d+1,n}(x)-x^{d+1}|

\displaystyle=|\Rc^{\times}\Dc(G_{d,n}(x),x)[n]-G_{d,n}(x)x+G_{d,n}(x)x-x^{d+1}|

\displaystyle\leq|\Rc^{\times}\Dc(G_{d,n}(x),x)[n]-G_{d,n}(x)x|+|G_{d,n}(x)x-x^{d+1}|

\displaystyle\leq 2^{-2n-1}+|G_{d,n}(x)-x^{d}||x|

\displaystyle\leq 2^{-2n-1}+d2^{-2n-1}\cdot 1

\displaystyle=(d+1)2^{-2n-1},

where we used the induction hypothesis in the last inequality.

This concludes the proof of the first statement. The second statement is a direct consequence of Lemma
C.2
.
∎

Summing the monomial estimates gives the corresponding bound for dyadic polynomials.

{lemma}

Let
P:x\mapsto a_{0}x^{0}+a_{1}x^{1}+\cdots+a_{d}x^{d}
be a polynomial with coefficients
a_{0},\ldots,a_{d}\in\Db
. For all
n\in\No
and
x\in[-1,1]
, we have

|F_{P,n}(x)|\leq\|P\|_{1}.

and

|F_{P,n}(x)-P(x)|\leq\|P\|_{1}\|P\|_{0}2^{-2n-1}.

Proof.

Let
P:x\mapsto a_{0}x^{0}+a_{1}x^{1}+\cdots+a_{d}x^{d}
be a polynomial with coefficients
a_{0},\ldots,a_{d}\in\Db
. Let also
n\in\No
and
x\in[-1,1]
. We have

|F_{P,n}(x)|=\left|\sum_{i=0}^{d}a_{i}G_{i,n}(x)\right|\leq\sum_{i=0}^{d}|a_{i}||G_{i,n}(x)|\leq\sum_{i=0}^{d}|a_{i}|=\|P\|_{1},

where we used Lemma
D
in the second inequality. Moreover, we have

\displaystyle|F_{P,n}(x)-P(x)|

\displaystyle=\left|\sum_{i=0}^{d}a_{i}G_{i,n}(x)-\sum_{i=0}^{d}a_{i}x^{i}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i=0}^{d}|a_{i}||G_{i,n}(x)-x^{i}|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i=0}^{d}|a_{i}|i2^{-2n-1}

\displaystyle\leq\|P\|_{1}\|P\|_{0}2^{-2n-1},

where we used Lemma
D
in the second inequality, and the fact that
i\leq d\leq\|P\|_{0}
for all
i\in\{0,\ldots,d\}
in the last inequality. This concludes the proof.
∎

Appendix E
Lower bounds on convergence speed

This appendix gathers lower-bound estimates showing that the runtime dependence
in the RNN approximation paradigm cannot be removed in general.

E.1
Fixed-time lower bound for the squaring function

We shall use the following elementary terminology. A continuous piecewise-affine
function
g:[-1,1]\to\R
has at most
N
breakpoints if there exist

-1=x_{0}<x_{1}<\cdots<x_{N}<x_{N+1}=1

such that
g
is affine on each interval
[x_{j},x_{j+1}]
.

We begin by recording that a fixed-time RNN realization is piecewise affine with controlled breakpoints.

{lemma}

Let
\Rc=(1,m,1;A_{h},b_{h},A_{x},A_{o},b_{o})
be a scalar-input scalar-output RNN,
and let
t_{0}\in\No
. Then the map

x\in[-1,1]\longmapsto(\Rc\Dc x)[t_{0}]

is continuous piecewise-affine and has at most
(m+1)^{t_{0}+1}-1

breakpoints.

Proof.

For
t\in\No
, write

h_{t}(x):=(\Hc\Dc x)[t].

We prove by induction that each coordinate of
h_{t}
is affine on every
interval of a partition of
[-1,1]
with at most
(m+1)^{t+1}
intervals.

For
t=0
, we have

h_{0}(x)=\rho(A_{x}x+b_{h}).

The
m
scalar affine functions appearing before the ReLU have at most

m
zeros in total. These zeros partition
[-1,1]
into at most
m+1

intervals, and on each such interval every coordinate of
h_{0}
is affine.

Assume that the claim holds at time
t-1
. On each interval of the
corresponding partition, each coordinate of
h_{t-1}
is affine. Therefore
each coordinate of

A_{h}h_{t-1}(x)+b_{h}

is affine on that interval and has at most one zero there. Adding the zeros
of these
m
affine functions refines the interval into at most
m+1

subintervals. Thus the number of intervals is multiplied by at most
m+1
,
and on every resulting interval

h_{t}(x)=\rho(A_{h}h_{t-1}(x)+b_{h})

is affine. This proves that
h_{t_{0}}
is affine on at most

(m+1)^{t_{0}+1}
intervals. Since
(\Rc\Dc x)[t_{0}]=A_{o}h_{t_{0}}(x)+b_{o}
, the
same partition works for the output map, which therefore has at most

(m+1)^{t_{0}+1}-1
breakpoints.
∎

The lower-bound argument needs a simple counting estimate for approximating a quadratic by piecewise-affine functions.

{lemma}

Let
\varepsilon>0
, and let
g:[-1,1]\to\R
be continuous
piecewise-affine. If

\sup_{x\in[-1,1]}|g(x)-x^{2}|\leq\varepsilon,

then
g
has at least
\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\varepsilon}}-1
breakpoints.

Proof.

We first record a local obstruction. Let
I=[a,b]\subset[-1,1]
have length

\ell=b-a
, and let
\lambda
be affine on
I
. Denote by
m=(a+b)/2
the
midpoint of
I
and put
r=\ell/2
. Define

e(y):=(m+y)^{2}-\lambda(m+y),\qquad y\in[-r,r].

Since
\lambda
is affine, there exist
\alpha,\beta\in\R
such that

e(y)=y^{2}+\alpha y+\beta.

In particular,

e(0)=\beta,\qquad e(r)=r^{2}+\alpha r+\beta,\qquad e(-r)=r^{2}-\alpha r+\beta,

yielding

\frac{e(r)+e(-r)}{2}-e(0)=r^{2}.

If
E:=\sup_{x\in I}|x^{2}-\lambda(x)|
, then

|e(-r)|,|e(0)|,|e(r)|\leq E
. Therefore

r^{2}=\left|\frac{e(r)+e(-r)}{2}-e(0)\right|\leq\frac{|e(r)|+|e(-r)|}{2}+|e(0)|\leq 2E.

Since
r=\ell/2
, we obtain
E\geq r^{2}/2=\ell^{2}/8
. Hence every affine

\lambda
satisfies

\sup_{x\in I}|x^{2}-\lambda(x)|\geq\frac{\ell^{2}}{8}.

Suppose now that
g
has
N
breakpoints. Then
[-1,1]
is decomposed into

N+1
intervals
I_{0},\ldots,I_{N}
on which
g
is affine. Fix one of these
intervals, say
I_{j}
, and denote its length by
\ell_{j}
. Since
g
is affine
on
I_{j}
, the local estimate above can be applied with

\lambda=g_{\mid I_{j}}
. Hence

\frac{\ell_{j}^{2}}{8}\leq\sup_{x\in I_{j}}|x^{2}-g(x)|\leq\sup_{x\in[-1,1]}|x^{2}-g(x)|\leq\varepsilon.

Therefore
\ell_{j}\leq\sqrt{8\varepsilon}
. Since this holds for every

j\in\{0,\ldots,N\}
and since the lengths of the intervals sum to
2
, we
get

N+1\geq\frac{2}{\sqrt{8\varepsilon}}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\varepsilon}},

as claimed.
∎

Combining Lemmas
E.1
and

E.1
shows the following. If an RNN

\Rc
with scalar input and scalar output satisfies

\sup_{x\in[-1,1]}|(\Rc\Dc x)[t_{0}]-x^{2}|\leq\varepsilon

for some
\varepsilon>0
, then

(m(\Rc)+1)^{t_{0}+1}-1\geq\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\varepsilon}}-1,

and hence

m(\Rc)\geq\left(\frac{1}{2\varepsilon}\right)^{\frac{1}{2(t_{0}+1)}}-1.

E.2
Minimax errors over compact classes

We now introduce the notation used for minimax lower bounds. For

m\in
and
B\geq 0
, let

\mathfrak{R}_{m,B}:=\left\{\Rc=(1,m^{\prime},1;A_{h},b_{h},A_{x},A_{o},b_{o})\,:\,m^{\prime}\leq m,\ {\Mc(\Rc)}\leq B\right\}.

Thus
\mathfrak{R}_{m,B}
is the class of scalar-input scalar-output RNNs whose
hidden state size is at most
m
and whose weights have magnitude at most
B
.
For
t\in\No
and
\Rc\in\mathfrak{R}_{m,B}
, we define

\Phi_{t}(\Rc):[-1,1]\to\R,\qquad\Phi_{t}(\Rc)(x):=(\Rc\Dc x)[t].

Equivalently, the set of functions realized at time
t
by RNNs in

\mathfrak{R}_{m,B}
is

\mathcal{A}_{t}(m,B):=\left\{\Phi_{t}(\Rc)\,:\,\Rc\in\mathfrak{R}_{m,B}\right\}\subset C([-1,1]).

Let
\Xc\subset C([-1,1])
be compact for the uniform norm

\|f\|_{\infty}:=\sup_{x\in[-1,1]}|f(x)|.

The fixed-time minimax error of the class
\mathfrak{R}_{m,B}
over
\Xc
is
defined by

\mathcal{E}_{t}(\Xc;m,B):=\sup_{f\in\Xc}\inf_{\Rc\in\mathfrak{R}_{m,B}}\sup_{x\in[-1,1]}\left|(\Rc\Dc x)[t]-f(x)\right|.

In terms of the realized function class
\mathcal{A}_{t}(m,B)
, this is simply

\mathcal{E}_{t}(\Xc;m,B)=\sup_{f\in\Xc}\inf_{g\in\mathcal{A}_{t}(m,B)}\|g-f\|_{\infty}.

The lower bounds below will estimate this quantity from below as a function of
the time
t
, the hidden state size
m
, and the weight magnitude
B
. We next recall the metric-entropy language used to formulate these lower bounds.

{definition}
[Covering number and metric entropy]

Let
\Yc\subset C([-1,1])
be a compact set and
\varepsilon>0
. We define the
\varepsilon
-covering
number of
\Yc
by

\mathcal{N}(\varepsilon,\Yc):=\inf\left\{N\in\,:\,\exists g_{1},\ldots,g_{N}\in C([-1,1]),\ \Yc\subset\bigcup_{j=1}^{N}\overline{B}_{\infty}(g_{j},\varepsilon)\right\},

where

\overline{B}_{\infty}(g,\varepsilon):=\left\{h\in C([-1,1])\,:\,\|h-g\|_{\infty}\leq\varepsilon\right\}.

The metric entropy of
\Yc
at scale
\varepsilon
is

\mathcal{H}(\varepsilon,\Yc):=\log\mathcal{N}(\varepsilon,\Yc).

We next define the parameter-to-realization map.

{definition}
[Parameter-to-realization map]

Let
m\in
and
t\in\No
, and set

p_{m}:=m^{2}+3m+1.

We identify
\R^{p_{m}}
with

\R^{m\times m}\times\R^{m}\times\R^{m\times 1}\times\R^{1\times m}\times\R.

For

\theta=(A_{h},b_{h},A_{x},A_{o},b_{o})\in\R^{p_{m}},

let

\Rc_{\theta}:=(1,m,1;A_{h},b_{h},A_{x},A_{o},b_{o}),

and define

F_{t,m}:\R^{p_{m}}\to C([-1,1]),\qquad F_{t,m}(\theta)(x):=\Phi_{t}(\Rc_{\theta})(x).

Note that Definition
E.2
gives, for
every
B\geq 0
,

\mathcal{A}_{t}(m,B)=F_{t,m}\left([-B,B]^{p_{m}}\right).

The following lemma gives Lipschitz control of this realization map with respect to the parameters.

{lemma}

Let
m\in
,
B\geq 1
, and
t\in\No
. Define

\Lambda_{t,m,B}:=8B^{2}m^{2}(2mB)^{2t}.

Then
F_{t,m}
is
\Lambda_{t,m,B}
-Lipschitz from

([-B,B]^{m^{2}+3m+1},\|\cdot\|_{\infty})
to

(C([-1,1]),\|\cdot\|_{\infty})
.

Proof.

Let
\theta,\theta^{\prime}\in[-B,B]^{m^{2}+3m+1}
be two parameter vectors, and let

\delta:=\|\theta-\theta^{\prime}\|_{\infty}.

We write
\Rc,\Rc^{\prime}
for the corresponding RNNs and

h_{s}(x):=(\Hc\Dc x)[s],\qquad h^{\prime}_{s}(x):=(\Hc^{\prime}\Dc x)[s],\qquad s\in\No.

Set

S_{t,m,B}:=\sum_{j=0}^{t}(mB)^{j}\qquad\text{and}\qquad H_{t,m,B}:=2BS_{t,m,B}.

First we bound the size of the hidden states. First note that

\|h_{0}(x)\|_{\infty}=\|\rho(A_{h}h_{-1}(x)+A_{x}(\Dc x)[0]+b_{h})\|_{\infty}=\|\rho(A_{x}x+b_{h})\|_{\infty}\leq\|A_{x}x\|_{\infty}+\|b_{h}\|_{\infty}\leq B|x|+B\leq 2B,

for every
x\in[-1,1]
. Similarly,
\|h^{\prime}_{0}(x)\|_{\infty}\leq 2B
.
Moreover, for every
s\in\{1,\ldots,t\}
and
x\in[-1,1]
we have

\displaystyle\|h_{s}(x)\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle=\|\rho(A_{h}h_{s-1}(x)+A_{x}(\Dc x)[s]+b_{h})\|_{\infty}=\|\rho(A_{h}h_{s-1}(x)+b_{h})\|_{\infty}\leq\|A_{h}h_{s-1}(x)\|_{\infty}+\|b_{h}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq mB\|h_{s-1}(x)\|_{\infty}+B\leq mB\|h_{s-1}(x)\|_{\infty}+2B.

It follows by induction that

\|h_{s}(x)\|_{\infty}\leq 2B\sum_{j=0}^{s}(mB)^{j}\leq H_{t,m,B},\qquad s\in\{0,\ldots,t\},

(208)

and we have the same bound for
h^{\prime}_{s}(x)
.
We now estimate the sensitivity of the hidden state to the parameters. Put

D_{s}:=\sup_{x\in[-1,1]}\|h_{s}(x)-h^{\prime}_{s}(x)\|_{\infty}.

Using the
1
-Lipschitz property of
\rho
, for every

s\in\{0,\ldots,t\}
and every
x\in[-1,1]
, we have

\displaystyle\|h_{s}(x)-h^{\prime}_{s}(x)\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\|A_{h}h_{s-1}(x)+A_{x}(\Dc x)[s]+b_{h}-A^{\prime}_{h}h^{\prime}_{s-1}(x)-A^{\prime}_{x}(\Dc x)[s]-b^{\prime}_{h}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\|A^{\prime}_{h}(h_{s-1}(x)-h^{\prime}_{s-1}(x))\|_{\infty}+\|(A_{h}-A^{\prime}_{h})h_{s-1}(x)\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\quad+\|(A_{x}-A^{\prime}_{x})(\Dc x)[s]\|_{\infty}+\|b_{h}-b^{\prime}_{h}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq mBD_{s-1}+mH_{t,m,B}\delta+\delta+\delta.

Taking the supremum over
x\in[-1,1]
gives

D_{s}\leq mBD_{s-1}+(mH_{t,m,B}+2)\delta,

with
D_{-1}=0
. Therefore

D_{t}\leq(mH_{t,m,B}+2)\delta\sum_{j=0}^{t}(mB)^{j}.

(209)

Finally, for the output layer,

|(\Rc\Dc x)[t]-(\Rc^{\prime}\Dc x)[t]|\leq Bm\|h_{t}(x)-h^{\prime}_{t}(x)\|_{\infty}+mH_{t,m,B}\delta+\delta.

Combining this with (
209
) gives

\displaystyle\|F_{t,m}(\theta)-F_{t,m}(\theta^{\prime})\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\left[1+Bm(mH_{t,m,B}+2)\sum_{j=0}^{t}(mB)^{j}+mH_{t,m,B}\right]\delta\leq\Lambda_{t,m,B}\delta.

∎

This Lipschitz control converts parameter dimension into a covering-number bound.

{lemma}

Let
m\in
,
B\geq 1
,
t\in\No
, and
\varepsilon>0
. Then

\mathcal{N}\left(\varepsilon,\mathcal{A}_{t}(m,B)\right)\leq\left(1+\frac{2B\Lambda_{t,m,B}}{\varepsilon}\right)^{m^{2}+3m+1}.

Proof.

Let
p:=m^{2}+3m+1
. The cube
[-B,B]^{p}
can be covered, for the

\ell^{\infty}
norm, by at most

\left(1+\frac{2B\Lambda_{t,m,B}}{\varepsilon}\right)^{p}

balls of radius
\varepsilon/\Lambda_{t,m,B}
. By Lemma

E.2
, the image under
F_{t,m}
of each such
ball is contained in a ball of radius
\varepsilon
in
C([-1,1])
. Since

\mathcal{A}_{t}(m,B)\subset F_{t,m}([-B,B]^{p})
by Definition

E.2
, this yields the claimed covering
bound for
\mathcal{A}_{t}(m,B)
.
∎

The next comparison turns small uniform approximation error into an entropy inequality.

{lemma}

Let
\Xc\subset C([-1,1])
be compact, let
m\in
,
B\geq 1
,
and
t\in\No
. Then

\mathcal{E}_{t}(\Xc;m,B)\geq\inf\left\{\varepsilon>0\,:\,\mathcal{N}(2\varepsilon,\Xc)\leq\mathcal{N}\left(\varepsilon,\mathcal{A}_{t}(m,B)\right)\right\}.

Proof.

Let
\eta>\mathcal{E}_{t}(\Xc;m,B)
. By definition of

\mathcal{E}_{t}(\Xc;m,B)
, for every
f\in\Xc
there exists

g_{f}\in\mathcal{A}_{t}(m,B)
such that

\|f-g_{f}\|_{\infty}<\eta.

Let
g_{1},\ldots,g_{M}
be an
\eta
-covering of
\mathcal{A}_{t}(m,B)
, with

M=\mathcal{N}(\eta,\mathcal{A}_{t}(m,B))
. For each
f\in\Xc
, choose

j\in\{1,\ldots,M\}
such that
\|g_{f}-g_{j}\|_{\infty}\leq\eta
. Then

\|f-g_{j}\|_{\infty}\leq\|f-g_{f}\|_{\infty}+\|g_{f}-g_{j}\|_{\infty}<2\eta.

Hence
\{g_{1},\ldots,g_{M}\}
is a
2\eta
-covering of
\Xc
, and so

\mathcal{N}(2\eta,\Xc)\leq\mathcal{N}\left(\eta,\mathcal{A}_{t}(m,B)\right).

Therefore for every
\eta>\mathcal{E}_{t}(\Xc;m,B)
, we also have

\eta\in\left\{\varepsilon>0\,:\,\mathcal{N}(2\varepsilon,\Xc)\leq\mathcal{N}\left(\varepsilon,\mathcal{A}_{t}(m,B)\right)\right\}.

Taking the infimum over
\eta>\mathcal{E}_{t}(\Xc;m,B)
gives the claim.
∎

Combining the two entropy estimates gives a necessary condition for minimax approximation.

{corollary}

Let
\Xc\subset C([-1,1])
be compact, let
m\in
,
B\geq 1
,
and
t\in\No
. Then

\mathcal{E}_{t}(\Xc;m,B)\geq\frac{1}{2}\inf\left\{\varepsilon\in(0,1]\,:\,\mathcal{N}(\varepsilon,\Xc)\leq\left(\frac{(2mB)^{2t+7}}{\varepsilon}\right)^{5m^{2}}\right\}

Proof.

Let

p:=m^{2}+3m+1

and

\mathcal{S}:=\left\{\varepsilon\in(0,1]\,:\,\mathcal{N}(\varepsilon,\Xc)\leq\left(\frac{(2mB)^{2t+7}}{\varepsilon}\right)^{5m^{2}}\right\}.

By Lemma
E.2
, for every

\varepsilon>0
,

\mathcal{N}\left(\varepsilon,\mathcal{A}_{t}(m,B)\right)\leq\left(1+\frac{2B\Lambda_{t,m,B}}{\varepsilon}\right)^{p}.

Since
m\geq 1
and
B\geq 1
, we have

p=m^{2}+3m+1\leq 5m^{2}

and

2B\Lambda_{t,m,B}=16B^{3}m^{2}(2mB)^{2t}\leq(2mB)^{2t+5}.

Therefore

\mathcal{N}\left(\varepsilon,\mathcal{A}_{t}(m,B)\right)\leq\left(1+\frac{(2mB)^{2t+5}}{\varepsilon}\right)^{5m^{2}}.

Hence, if
\rho\in(0,1]
and

\mathcal{N}(\rho,\Xc)\leq\mathcal{N}\left(\frac{\rho}{2},\mathcal{A}_{t}(m,B)\right),

then

\mathcal{N}(\rho,\Xc)\leq\left(\frac{(2mB)^{2t+6}}{\rho/2}\right)^{5m^{2}}\leq\left(\frac{(2mB)^{2t+7}}{\rho}\right)^{5m^{2}}.

Hence

\left\{\rho\in(0,1]\,:\,\mathcal{N}(\rho,\Xc)\leq\mathcal{N}\left(\frac{\rho}{2},\mathcal{A}_{t}(m,B)\right)\right\}\subset\mathcal{S}.

If
\mathcal{E}_{t}(\Xc;m,B)\geq 1/2
, then the claim is immediate. Assume

\mathcal{E}_{t}(\Xc;m,B)<1/2
. The proof of Lemma

E.2
shows that every

\eta>\mathcal{E}_{t}(\Xc;m,B)
satisfies

\mathcal{N}(2\eta,\Xc)\leq\mathcal{N}\left(\eta,\mathcal{A}_{t}(m,B)\right).

Thus every
2\eta
with

\eta\in(2\mathcal{E}_{t}(\Xc;m,B),1/2]
belongs to
\mathcal{S}
, and so

\inf\mathcal{S}\leq 2\mathcal{E}_{t}(\Xc;m,B).

This proves the claim.
∎

We now spell out what Corollary

E.2
gives for the function classes
used in the main text. Throughout this subsection, we fix
m\in
,
B\geq 1
,
and put

A_{t,m,B}:=5m^{2}(2t+7)\log(2mB),\qquad C_{m}:=5m^{2}.

With this notation, the condition appearing in Corollary

E.2
can be written as

\log\mathcal{N}(\varepsilon,\Xc)\leq A_{t,m,B}+C_{m}\log(\varepsilon^{-1}).

(210)

Thus the minimax lower bound is obtained by inverting the metric entropy of

\Xc
. The following theorem gives a convenient rate-level inversion, avoiding
the exact transcendental formulas.

{theorem}
[Entropy inversion]
Let
\Xc\subset C([-1,1])
be compact. Let

h:(0,1]\to(0,\infty)
be nonincreasing and assume that

\log(\varepsilon^{-1})=o(h(\varepsilon))\qquad\text{and}\qquad\log\mathcal{N}(\varepsilon,\Xc)\geq h(\varepsilon),\quad\varepsilon\in(0,1].

(211)

Then there exist constants
K>0
and
t_{0}\in
, depending only on

m,B
and
h
, such that, for every
t\geq t_{0}
,

\mathcal{E}_{t}(\Xc;m,B)\geq\frac{1}{2}h^{-1}(K(t+1)),

where
h^{-1}(T):=\inf\left\{\varepsilon\in(0,1]\,:\,h(\varepsilon)\leq T\right\}
.

Proof.

Since
\log(\varepsilon^{-1})=o(h(\varepsilon))
, there exists

\varepsilon_{1}\in(0,1]
such that

C_{m}\log(\varepsilon^{-1})\leq\frac{1}{2}h(\varepsilon),\qquad\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{1}].

(212)

Let
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{1}]
satisfy
(
210
). Combining
(
210
), (
211
) and
(
212
), we obtain

h(\varepsilon)\leq A_{t,m,B}+C_{m}\log(\varepsilon^{-1})\leq A_{t,m,B}+\frac{1}{2}h(\varepsilon),

hence

h(\varepsilon)\leq 2A_{t,m,B}.

Choose
K>0
such that

2A_{t,m,B}\leq K(t+1),\qquad t\in\No.

Then every
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{1}]
satisfying
(
210
) also satisfies

\varepsilon\geq h^{-1}(K(t+1)).

Since
h^{-1}(K(t+1))\to 0
as
t\to\infty
, there exists
t_{0}\in
such
that
h^{-1}(K(t+1))\leq\varepsilon_{1}
for every
t\geq t_{0}
. For such

t
, the same lower bound is automatic for admissible scales

\varepsilon>\varepsilon_{1}
. Therefore

\inf\left\{\varepsilon\in(0,1]\,:\,\mathcal{N}(\varepsilon,\Xc)\leq\left(\frac{(2mB)^{2t+7}}{\varepsilon}\right)^{5m^{2}}\right\}\geq h^{-1}(K(t+1)).

Corollary
E.2
proves the claim.
∎
